\n{{Clear}}\n\n==Standings==\nThe point system consists of outcome based scoring and bonuses for an early win. Under the outcome based scoring system, the winner of a fight receives 3 points and the loser receives 0 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. For example, if a fighter wins a fight in the first round, then the fighter will receive 6 total points. If a fighter misses weight, then the fighter that missed weight will receive 0 points and his opponent will receive 3 points due to a walkover victory.{{cite web|url=http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|title=Rules and Regulations|publisher=PFL|access-date=2018-08-23|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180808170826/http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|archive-date=2018-08-08|url-status=dead}}\n\n===Featherweight===\n

\n\n\u265b = Clinched playoff spot ---\n\ud83d\udeab = Ruled out ---\nE = Eliminated\n\n''NOTE: Prior to [[PFL 8 (2018 season)|PFL 8]], which started the PFL playoff, [[Valdrin Istrefi]] was ruled out because of an injury. Caio Alencar was declared the first alternate and participated in the playoff.''\n\n==See also==\n*[[List of PFL events]]\n*[[List of current PFL fighters]]\n\n==References==\n{{Reflist}}\n\n{{World Series of Fighting Events}}\n{{WSOF Title Holders}}\n{{2018 in mixed martial arts}}\n\n[[Category:Professional Fighters League by year|2018]]\n[[Category:2018 in mixed martial arts]]"}}}]},"66876513":{"pageid":66876513,"ns":0,"title":"2019 in Professional Fighters League","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","*":"{{Infobox television\n| italic_title = no\n| image =\n| genre = [[Broadcasting of sports events|Sports]]\n| country = [[United States]]\n| network = [[ESPN]]\n| first_aired = {{Start date|2019|05|09}}\n| last_aired = {{End date|2019|12|31}}\n}}\n\nThis is a list of events and standings for the [[Professional Fighters League]], a mixed martial arts organization based in the United States, for the 2019 season.\n\nEach weight class had 12 fighters, except the women's class which had 8 fighters. Each fighter faced two rivals in the regular season. The top fighters in each weight class (8 in the men's classes, 4 in the women's class) qualified to the playoffs. The season concluded on December 31, 2019 with six championship bouts back-to-back with a $10 million prize pool.\n\n==2019 world champions==\n{|class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!Division!!Upper weight limit!!Champion!!Date\n|-\n|[[Heavyweight (MMA)|Heavyweight]]||265 lb ''(120 kg; 18.9 st)''|| [[Ali Isayev]] || December 31, 2019 (''[[PFL 10 (2019 season)|PFL 10]]'')\n|-\n|[[Light Heavyweight (MMA)|Light Heavyweight]]||205 lb ''(93 kg; 14.6 st)''|| [[Emiliano Sordi]] || December 31, 2019 (''[[PFL 10 (2019 season)|PFL 10]]'')\n|-\n|[[Welterweight (MMA)|Welterweight]]||170 lb ''(77 kg; 12 st)''|| [[Ray Cooper III]] || December 31, 2019 (''[[PFL 10 (2019 season)|PFL 10]]'')\n|-\n|[[Lightweight (MMA)|Lightweight]]||155 lb ''(70 kg; 11.1 st)''|| [[Natan Schulte]] || December 31, 2019 (''[[PFL 10 (2019 season)|PFL 10]]'')\n|-\n|[[Lightweight (MMA)|Women's Lightweight]]||155 lb ''(70 kg; 11.1 st)''|| [[Kayla Harrison]] || December 31, 2019 (''[[PFL 10 (2019 season)|PFL 10]]'')\n|-\n|[[Featherweight (MMA)|Featherweight]]||145 lb ''(66 kg; 10.5 st)''|| [[Lance Palmer]] || December 31, 2019 (''[[PFL 10 (2019 season)|PFL 10]]'')\n|}\n\n==Events==\n{| class=\"sortable wikitable succession-box\" style=\"font-size:90%;\"\n! \n{{Clear}}\n\n==Standings==\nThe PFL points system is based on results of the match. The winner of a fight receives 3 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. The bonus for winning in the third round requires a fight be stopped before 4:59 of the third round. No bonus point will be awarded if a fighter wins via decision. For example, if a fighter wins a fight in the first round, then the fighter will receive 6 total points. A decision win will result in three total points. If a fighter misses weight, the opponent (should they comply with weight limits) will receive 3 points due to a walkover victory, regardless of winning or losing the bout; if the non-offending fighter subsequently wins with a stoppage, all bonus points will be awarded.{{cite web|url=http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|title=Rules and Regulations|publisher=PFL|access-date=2019-05-25|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180808170826/http://www.pflmma.com/rules-regulations/|archive-date=2018-08-08|url-status=dead}}\n\n===Heavyweight===\n

The late additions to the season were the welterweights [[Gleison Tibau]], [[Alexey Kunchenko]] and Jason Ponet, featherweights [[Chris Wade (fighter)|Chris Wade]] and Anthony Dizy, women\u2019s lightweights [[Kaitlin Young]] and Taylor Guardado and light heavyweight Nick Roehrick.{{cite web |last1=MMA Fighting Newswire |title=PFL finalizes 2021 roster with additional signings including UFC vet Gleison Tibau, Invicta FC's Kaitlin Young |url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2021/2/25/22301428/pfl-finalizes-2021-roster-signings-including-ufc-vet-gleison-tibau-and-invicta-fcs-kaitlin-young |website=mmafighting.com |date=25 February 2021 |access-date=25 February 2021}}\n\n==2021 world champions==\n{|class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!Division!!Upper weight limit!!Champion!!Date\n|-\n|[[Heavyweight (MMA)|Heavyweight]]||265 lb ''(120 kg; 18.9 st)''|| [[Bruno Cappelozza]]|| October 27, 2021 (''[[PFL 10 (2021 season)|PFL 10]]'')\n|-\n|[[Light Heavyweight (MMA)|Light Heavyweight]]||205 lb ''(93 kg; 14.6 st)''|| [[Ant\u00f4nio Carlos J\u00fanior]] || October 27, 2021 (''[[PFL 10 (2021 season)|PFL 10]]'')\n|-\n|[[Welterweight (MMA)|Welterweight]]||170 lb ''(77 kg; 12 st)''|| [[Ray Cooper III]] || October 27, 2021 (''[[PFL 10 (2021 season)|PFL 10]]'')\n|-\n|[[Lightweight (MMA)|Lightweight]]||155 lb ''(70 kg; 11.1 st)''|| [[Raush Manfio]] || October 27, 2021 (''[[PFL 10 (2021 season)|PFL 10]]'')\n|-\n|[[Lightweight (MMA)|Women's Lightweight]]||155 lb ''(70 kg; 11.1 st)''|| [[Kayla Harrison]] || October 27, 2021 (''[[PFL 10 (2021 season)|PFL 10]]'')\n|-\n|[[Featherweight (MMA)|Featherweight]]||145 lb ''(66 kg; 10.5 st)''|| [[Movlid Khaybulaev]] || October 27, 2021 (''[[PFL 10 (2021 season)|PFL 10]]'')\n|}\n\n==Rule Changes==\n\nA fighter who is overweight on the scale at weigh-ins will be deducted one point in the standings in the PFL's season structure. In previous seasons, a fighter who missed weight could not earn any points even in a victory, but no standings points were actually taken away until now.\n\nIn 2021, the top four fighters per division after the regular season will make the playoffs, a number down from the eight who made the playoffs in men's divisions in previous seasons. The women's lightweight division will have the top four make the playoffs the way it did in 2019, the PFL's most recent season. The four athletes making the playoffs will advance directly to the semifinals in each division. There will be no quarterfinals, nor a situation where a fighter must win twice in a single night.{{cite web |last1=Raimondi |first1=Marc |title=PFL makes rule changes ahead of 2021 season |url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/31177642/pfl-makes-rule-format-changes-ahead-2021-season |website=ESPN.com |access-date=1 April 2021 |language=en |date=1 April 2021}}\n\n==Events==\nThe first event of the 2021 PFL season will kick off on April 23, 2021. The prelims were broadcast on [[ESPN+]], while the main card was broadcast on [[ESPN2]].{{cite web |last1=Simon |first1=Zane |title=PFL announces 2021 season debut for April 23 on ESPN 2 |url=https://www.bloodyelbow.com/2021/2/2/22262827/professional-fighters-league-pfl-2021-first-event-anthony-pettis-debut-mma-news |website=bloodyelbow.com |date=2 February 2021 |access-date=25 February 2021 }}{{Dead link|date=September 2026 |bot=InternetArchiveBot }} All of the regular season events were held in the [[Atlantic City, New Jersey]], at the [[Ocean Casino Resort]] in a \u201cbubble environment\u201d. All fighters will undergo a 17-day quarantine period, as a precaution for [[COVID-19]].{{cite web |last1=MMA Fighting Newswire |title=PFL announces Atlantic City venue for 2021 regular season events, 'bubble environment' quarantine protocol |url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2021/3/4/22313412/pfl-announces-atlantic-city-venue-2021-regular-season-events-bubble-environment-quarantine-protocol |website=mmafighting.com |date=4 March 2021 |access-date=5 March 2021}}\n\nThe trio of events constituting the season playoffs will be held at the [[Seminole Hard Rock Hotel & Casino Hollywood]] in [[Hollywood, Florida]].{{cite web |last1=Fontana |first1=Scott |title=PFL playoffs slated for August in Florida casino |url=https://nypost.com/2021/06/08/pfl-playoffs-slated-for-august-in-hollywood-fla/ |website=nypost.com |date=8 June 2021 |access-date=18 June 2021}}\n\n{| class=\"sortable wikitable succession-box\" style=\"font-size:90%;\"\n! \n{{Clear}}\n\n==Standings==\nThe PFL points system is based on results of the match. The winner of a fight receives 3 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. A no-contest will be scored as a draw. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. The bonus for winning in the third round requires a fight be stopped before 4:59 of the third round. No bonus point will be awarded if a fighter wins via decision. For example, if a fighter wins a fight in the first round, then the fighter will receive 6 total points. A decision win will result in three total points. If a fighter misses weight, the opponent (should they comply with weight limits) will receive 3 points due to a walkover victory, regardless of winning or losing the bout, with the fighter who missed weight being deducted 1 standings point; if the non-offending fighter subsequently wins with a stoppage, all bonus points will be awarded. A fighter who was unable to compete for any reason, will receive a 1-point\npenalty (-1 point in the standings). The fighters who made weight will not receive a walkover, but will earn points and contracted purse amounts based on their performance in the altered matchup.{{Cite web|title=2021 Official Rules|url=https://pflmma-prod.s3.amazonaws.com/assets/downloads/2021_Rules_and_Regulations_4-26-2021.pdf|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210507024409/https://pflmma-prod.s3.amazonaws.com/assets/downloads/2021_Rules_and_Regulations_4-26-2021.pdf |archive-date=2021-05-07 }}\n\n===Heavyweight===\nThe heavyweight roster included the current PFL Heavyweight champion [[Ali Isaev]], the former [[UFC]] Heavyweight champion [[Fabricio Werdum]], the former [[Jungle Fight]] champion [[Bruno Cappelozza]], former [[Absolute Championship Berkut|ACB]] champion [[Denis Goltsov]], [[M-1 Global]] title challenger Ante Delija, [[UFC]] veteran [[Justin Willis (martial artist)|Justin Willis]], Renan Ferreira, Hatef Moeil, Brandon Sayles and Mohammed Usman.{{cite web |last1=Holland |first1=Jesse |title=PFL announces heavyweight, women's lightweight rosters for 2021 season feat. Fabricio Werdum, Kayla Harrison |url=https://www.mmamania.com/2021/2/9/22274776/pfl-announces-heavyweight-womens-lightweight-rosters-2021-season-fabricio-werdum-kayla-harrison-mma |website=mmamania.com |date=9 February 2021 |access-date=25 February 2021}}\n\n

\n\n===Light Heavyweight===\nThe first nine light heavyweights announced for the 2021 season were the 2019 PFL champion [[Emiliano Sordi]], the former [[UFC]] and [[Glory (kickboxing)|Glory]] fighter [[Chris Camozzi]], [[UFC]] veterans [[Cezar Ferreira]], [[Jordan Johnson (fighter)|Jordan Johnson]], [[Daniel Spohn]] and [[Tom Lawlor]], Marthin Hamlet, [[Smealinho Rama]] and Jordan Young. Nick Roehrick was later added to the roster, while the former [[UFC]] middleweight [[Ant\u00f4nio Carlos J\u00fanior]] joined the roster on March 6, 2021.{{cite web |last1=Raimondi |first1=Marc |title=PFL signs UFC veteran Antonio Carlos Junior for 2021 season |url=https://www.espn.com/mma/story/_/id/31016737/pfl-signs-ufc-veteran-antonio-carlos-junior-2021-season |website=espn.com |date=6 March 2021 |access-date=7 March 2021}} PFL announced that [[Ant\u00f4nio Carlos J\u00fanior]] will replace [[Jordan Johnson (fighter)|Jordan Johnson]] for the whole season.{{cite web |last1=Morgan |first1=John |title=Antonio Carlos Junior signs with PFL, debuts against Tom Lawlor |url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2021/03/antonio-carlos-junior-signs-with-pfl-debut-against-tom-lawlor |website=mmajunkie.usatoday.com |date=6 March 2021 |access-date=7 April 2021}} At the beginning of April, [[Smealinho Rama]] was replaced by [[Vinny Magalh\u00e3es]] for the whole season.{{cite web |title=PFL MMA |url=https://twitter.com/ProFightLeague/status/1377691821283934209 |website=twitter.com |access-date=7 April 2021}} At weigh-ins of [[PFL 2 (2021 season)|PFL 2]], Vinny had to be taken to the hospital due to complications of his weight cut and was replaced by Askar Mozharov.{{Cite web|last=Martin|first=Damon|date=2021-04-28|title=Vinny Magalhaes out of PFL 2 after being taken to hospital, Askar Mozharov stepping in as replacement|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2021/4/28/22408287/vinny-magalhaes-out-of-pfl-2-after-being-taken-to-hospital-askar-mozharov-stepping-in-as-replacement|access-date=2021-04-28|website=MMA Fighting|language=en}}\n\n

\n\n===Welterweight===\nThe first seven welterweights announced for the 2021 season were the 2019 PFL champion [[Ray Cooper III]], former [[Bellator MMA|Bellator]] champion [[Rory MacDonald (fighter)|Rory MacDonald]], PFL veteran [[Magomed Magomedkerimov]], [[UFC]] veterans [[David Michaud]] and Jo\u00e3o Zeferino, Nikolai Aleksakhin and [[Sadibou Sy]].{{cite web |last1=MMAWeekly.com Staff |title=PFL unveils 2021 welterweight and light heavyweight rosters |url=https://www.mmaweekly.com/pfl-unveils-2021-welterweight-and-light-heavyweight-rosters |website=mmaweekly.com |date=4 February 2021 |access-date=25 February 2021}} [[Alexey Kunchenko]] was additionally announced for the 2021 season.{{cite web |last1=Hannoun |first1=Farah |title=Ex-UFC fighter Alexey Kunchenko inks deal with PFL, joins 2021 welterweight season |url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2021/02/ex-ufc-fighter-alexey-kunchenko-inks-deal-with-pfl-joins-2021-welterweight-season |website=mmajunkie.usatoday.com |date=19 February 2021 |access-date=25 February 2021}} Jason Ponet and former [[UFC]] Lightweight [[Gleison Tibau]] were later added on. After revealing a heart condition, [[David Michaud]] had to back out of the season and was replaced by [[Curtis Millender]].{{cite web |title=Curtis Millender steps in to fight Rory MacDonald at PFL 2021, Week 2 on April 29 |url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2021/04/pfl-2-curtis-millender-to-fight-rory-macdonald-replaces-david-michaud |website=MMA Junkie |access-date=9 April 2021 |date=9 April 2021}}\n\nSy replaced Zeferino because of an injury before the PFL semifinals.\n\n

Aubin-Mercier]], [[Clay Collard]], [[Marcin Held]] and [[Johnny Case]], as well as Akhmed Aliev, Joilton Lutterbach, Mikhail Odintsov and Loik Radzhabov.{{cite web |last1=Martin |first1=Damon \n\n===Women's Lightweight===\nThe women's 2021 roster included the 2019 PFL champion [[Kayla Harrison]], [[Cindy Dandois]], [[Genah Fabian]], Olena Kolesnyk, Mariana Morais, [[Larissa Pacheco]], Julija Pajic and Laura Sanchez. Kaitlin Young and Taylor Guardado rounded out the roster.\n\nPacheco was eliminated at the semifinals when she was two pounds over the lightweight limit.{{cite web | url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2021/8/18/22631285/larissa-pacheco-out-of-pfl-playoffs-after-missing-weight-mariana-morais-fight-taylor-guardado-pfl-8 | title=Larissa Pacheco out of PFL playoffs after missing weight, Mariana Morais to fight Taylor Guardado at PFL 8 | date=18 August 2021 }}\n\n

\n\n===Featherweight===\nEight fighters were initially announced for the 2021 season. The two-time PFL champion [[Lance Palmer]], UFC veteran [[Sheymon Moraes]], [[Bellator MMA|Bellator]] veteran [[Bubba Jenkins]], Tyler Diamond, Sung Bin Jo, [[Movlid Khaybulaev]], [[Brendan Loughnane]] and Jason Soares. Chris Wade, who previously competed in the PFL Lightweight division, was added to the featherweight roster. Anthony Dizy was later added as well.{{cite web |last1=Pettry |first1=Jay |title=PFL Completes 60-Fighter Roster for 2021 Season Including Gleison Tibau, Kaitlin Young |url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/PFL-Completes-60Fighter-Roster-for-2021-Season-Including-Gleison-Tibau-Kaitlin-Young-177890 |website=sherdog.com |access-date=26 February 2021}} Jason Soares was not medically cleared, hence he was replaced by Lazar Stojadinovic.{{cite web |title=Curtis Millender Joins PFL 2021 Tournament, Faces Rory MacDonald on April 29 |url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Curtis-Millender-Joins-PFL-2021-Tournament-Faces-Rory-MacDonald-on-April-29-178679 |website=Sherdog |access-date=9 April 2021}}\n\n

Young and up-and-coming male and female MMA prospects will compete for a slot in the PFL tournament season and a chance at $1 million. Each week, the PFL Challenger Series will consist of a celebrity guest panel featuring personalities in film, athletics, and music.{{Cite web|last=Newswire|first=MMA Fighting|date=2022-01-19|title=PFL Challenger Series debuts Feb. 18 on Fubo Sports Network|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2022/1/19/22891858/pfl-challenger-series-debuts-feb-18-on-fubo-sports-network|access-date=2022-01-20|website=MMA Fighting|language=en}}{{Cite web|last=Stitt|first=Anthony|title=PFL Challenger Series Gearing Up For Debut, With Undiscovered Talent Set To Get A Big Break|url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/anthonystitt/2022/01/10/pfl-challenger-series-gearing-up-for-debut---undiscovered-talent-to-get-big-break/|access-date=2022-01-20|website=Forbes|language=en}}{{Cite web|date=2021-12-14|title=PFL Announces \"Challenger Series\" To Scout Up-And-Coming Talent|url=https://www.mmanews.com/2021/12/pfl-announces-challenger-series-to-scout-up-and-coming-talent/|access-date=2022-01-20|website=MMA News}} The PFL Challenger Series will debut on [[fuboTV]]. The new deal includes \u201cexpanded media rights,\u201d with the entirety of the PFL's 2022 playoffs, as well as the \u201cmajority of regular season events\u201d to simulcast on ESPN and ESPN+. \n{{Clear}}\n\n==Standings==\nThe PFL points system is based on results of the match. The winner of a fight receives 3 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. A no-contest will be scored as a draw. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. The bonus for winning in the third round requires a fight be stopped before 4:59 of the third round. No bonus point will be awarded if a fighter wins via decision. For example, if a fighter wins a fight in the first round, then the fighter will receive 6 total points. A decision win will result in three total points. If a fighter misses weight, the opponent (should they comply with weight limits) will receive 3 points due to a walkover victory, regardless of winning or losing the bout, with the fighter who missed weight being deducted 1 standings point; if the non-offending fighter subsequently wins with a stoppage, all bonus points will be awarded. A fighter who was unable to compete for any reason, will receive a 1-point\npenalty (-1 point in the standings). The fighters who made weight will not receive a walkover, but will earn points and contracted purse amounts based on their performance in the altered matchup.{{Cite web|title=2021 Official Rules|url=https://pflmma-prod.s3.amazonaws.com/assets/downloads/2021_Rules_and_Regulations_4-26-2021.pdf|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210507024409/https://pflmma-prod.s3.amazonaws.com/assets/downloads/2021_Rules_and_Regulations_4-26-2021.pdf |archive-date=2021-05-07 }}\n\n===Heavyweight===\n{| class=\"wikitable sortable\"\n! PFL Challenger Series ==\n{{See also|PFL Challenger Series}}\n'''PFL Challenger Series''' is an American [[mixed martial arts]] promotion. Young and up-and-coming male and female MMA prospects will compete for a slot in the PFL tournament season and a chance at $1 million. Each week, the PFL Challenger Series will consist of a celebrity guest panel featuring personalities in film, athletics, and music.{{Cite web|last=Newswire|first=MMA Fighting|date=2022-01-19|title=PFL Challenger Series debuts Feb. 18 on Fubo Sports Network|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2022/1/19/22891858/pfl-challenger-series-debuts-feb-18-on-fubo-sports-network|access-date=2022-01-20|website=MMA Fighting|language=en}}{{Cite web|last=Stitt|first=Anthony|title=PFL Challenger Series Gearing Up For Debut, With Undiscovered Talent Set To Get A Big Break|url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/anthonystitt/2022/01/10/pfl-challenger-series-gearing-up-for-debut---undiscovered-talent-to-get-big-break/|access-date=2022-01-20|website=Forbes|language=en}}{{Cite web|date=2021-12-14|title=PFL Announces \"Challenger Series\" To Scout Up-And-Coming Talent|url=https://www.mmanews.com/2021/12/pfl-announces-challenger-series-to-scout-up-and-coming-talent/|access-date=2022-01-20|website=MMA News}} The PFL Challenger Series will debut on [[fuboTV]]. The eight events will stream on consecutive Friday nights starting Feb. 18, and run through March. It will also air on its linear network, [[Fubo Sports Network]].{{Cite web|date=2021-12-09|title=Everything you need to know about PFL's Challenger PFL Europe ==\nStarting in 2023, PFL Europe will feature the top emerging European MMA fighters, and will be broadcast during prime local hours with all events staged in Europe. The format will follow the same as the regular PFL Season, with the winner receiving a $100,000 prize and a chance to earn a spot in the 2024 PFL regular season.{{Cite web |title=PFL and DAZN announce historic media rights partnership for Europe |url=https://www.pflmma.com/news/pfl-and-dazn-announce-historic-media-rights-partnership-for-europe |website=Professional Fighters League}}{{Cite web \n\n{{Clear}}\n\n==Standings==\nThe PFL points system is based on results of the match. The winner of a fight receives 3 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. A no-contest will be scored as a draw. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. The bonus for winning in the third round requires a fight be stopped before 4:59 of the third round. No bonus point will be awarded if a fighter wins via decision. For example, if a fighter wins a fight in the first round, then the fighter will receive 6 total points. A decision win will result in three total points. If a fighter misses weight, the opponent (should they comply with weight limits) will receive 3 points due to a walkover victory, regardless of winning or losing the bout, with the fighter who missed weight being deducted 1 standings point; if the non-offending fighter subsequently wins with a stoppage, all bonus points will be awarded. A fighter who was unable to compete for any reason, will receive a 1-point\npenalty (-1 point in the standings). The fighters who made weight will not receive a walkover, but will earn points and contracted purse amounts based on their performance in the altered matchup.{{Cite web|title=2021 Official Rules|url=https://pflmma-prod.s3.amazonaws.com/assets/downloads/2021_Rules_and_Regulations_4-26-2021.pdf|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210507024409/https://pflmma-prod.s3.amazonaws.com/assets/downloads/2021_Rules_and_Regulations_4-26-2021.pdf |archive-date=2021-05-07 }}\n\nNine PFL fighters were suspended by the Nevada State Athletic Commission after failing drug tests; which included former UFC title challenger [[Thiago Santos]] and former PFL heavyweight champion [[Bruno Cappelozza]]. Additional fighters impacted by the suspensions are [[Krzysztof Jotko]], [[Rizvan Kuniev]], Mohammed Fakhreddine, [[Cezar Ferreira]], and Will Fleury. Two featherweight season fighters, Alejandro Flores and Daniel Torres, were also suspended. In light of fighter suspensions, they were all removed from the season after the first round of bouts.{{cite web |title=Nine PFL fighters suspended, scrambling season |url=https://www.espn.co.uk/mma/story/_/id/37613472/nine-pfl-fighters-suspended-nevada-state-athletic-commission |website=ESPN.com |access-date=14 May 2023 |language=en |date=12 May 2023}}\n\n===Heavyweight===\n{| class=\"wikitable sortable\"\n! replace it with the Women's Flyweight division.{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2024/01/pfl-2024-divisions-womens-flyweight-featherweight|title=PFL scraps women's featherweight division, scheduled to take part in the 2024 tournaments.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2024/2/20/24078153/pfl-fighters-dates-2024-regular-season-las-vegas-card-ufc-300|title=PFL announces first Runner-up\n|-\n| rowspan=4|[[PFL 10 (2024)]]\n| rowspan=4|Nov 29, 2024\n| Welterweight \n| {{flagicon|EGY}} Omar El-Dafrawy \n| {{flagicon|KUW}} Mohammad Alaqraa\n|-\n| Lightweight \n| {{flagicon|IRN}} [[Mohsen Mohammadseifi]] \n| {{flagicon|LBN}} Georges Eid\n|-\n| Featherweight \n| {{flagicon|SAU}} Abdullah Al-Qahtani \n| {{flagicon|MAR}} Maraoune Bellagouit\n|-\n| Bantamweight \n| {{flagicon|IRQ}} Ali Taleb \n| {{flagicon|MAR}} Rachid El Hazoume\n|}\n\n== Format ==\nThe PFL points system is based on results of the match. The winner of a fight receives 3 points. If the fight ends in a draw, both fighters will receive 1 point. A no-contest will be scored as a draw. The bonus for winning a fight in the first, second, or third round is 3 points, 2 points, and 1 point respectively. The bonus for winning in the third round requires a fight be stopped before 4:59 of the third round. No bonus point will be awarded if a fighter wins via decision. If a fighter misses weight, the opponent (should they comply with weight limits) will receive 3 points due to a walkover victory, regardless of winning or losing the bout, with the fighter who missed weight being deducted 1 point; if the non-offending fighter subsequently wins with a stoppage, all bonus points will be awarded. A fighter who was unable to compete for any reason, will receive a 1-point\npenalty (-1 point in the standings). The fighters who made weight will not receive a walkover, but will earn points and contracted purse amounts based on their performance in the altered matchup.{{Cite web|title=2021 Official Rules|url=https://pflmma-prod.s3.amazonaws.com/assets/downloads/2021_Rules_and_Regulations_4-26-2021.pdf|url-status=live|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210507024409/https://pflmma-prod.s3.amazonaws.com/assets/downloads/2021_Rules_and_Regulations_4-26-2021.pdf |archive-date=2021-05-07 }}\n\n== Standings by weight class ==\n===Heavyweight===\n{| class=\"wikitable sortable\"\n! first_date = {{Start date|2025|01|25}}\n| first_date_aired =\n| last_date = {{Start date|2025|12|20}}\n| last_date_aired =\n| total_events = \n| total_fights = \n| title_fights = \n| preceded_by = [[2024 in Professional Fighters League|2024 in PFL]]\n| followed_by = [[2026 in Professional Fighters League|2026 in PFL]]\n}}\n\nThe year '''2025''' was the 7th year in the history of the [[Professional Fighters League]] (PFL), a [[mixed martial arts]] promotion based in the United States.\n\n==Background==\nOn January 7, 2025, PFL chairman [[Donn Davis]] announced that elbows will be legal in the promotion, after the PFL had banned elbows for the regular season and playoffs. Elbows were typically allowed in non-season and non-playoff fights only.{{Cite web |date=2025-01-07 |title=Donn Davis announces that elbows now legal in all PFL bouts \n|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2025/01/pfl-elbows-legal-all-fights |access-date=2025-01-15 |website=MMA Junkie |language=en-US}}\n\nOn January 9, 2025, it was announced that the 2025 season of PFL would no longer operate as a points-based tournament, but would rather be contested as a single-elimination tournament.{{Cite web |date=2025-01-09 |title=PFL ditches $1 million winner, season points format for 2025 \n|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2025/01/pfl-changes-1-million-winner-season-points-format-2025-mma |access-date=2025-01-15 |website=MMA Junkie |language=en-US}}\n\nOn January 15, 2025, PFL revealed that hold eight tournaments in 2025, confirming the $500,000 pay for each winner. In total, it will distribute more than $20 million to the fighters. The tournaments will take place at heavyweight, light heavyweight, middleweight, welterweight, lightweight, featherweight, bantamweight and women's flyweight divisions.{{Cite web |last=Cruz |first=Guilherme |date=2025-01-15 |title=PFL reveals dates, details for new tournament format in 2025 \n|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2025/1/15/24344310/pfl-reveals-dates-details-new-tournament-format-2025 |access-date=2025-01-15 |website=MMA Fighting |language=en}}{{Cite web |date=2025-01-15 |title=PFL announces 2025 tournaments over eight weight classes with fighters TBD \n|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2025/01/pfl-2025-tournaments-eight-weight-classes |access-date=2025-01-15 |website=MMA Junkie |language=en-US}}{{Cite web |last=Holland |first=Jesse |date=2025-01-15 |title=New-look PFL announces 2025 World Tournament, start date and weight brackets \n|url=https://www.mmamania.com/2025/1/15/24344326/new-look-pfl-announces-2025-world-tournament-start-date-weight-brackets-mma |access-date=2025-01-15 |website=MMAmania.com |language=en}}\n\n==Events
